\left|{\partial_{z}^{n^{\prime}}\left(r^{(3)}[\Phi](x+z,x)\right)}\right|\lesssim_{A_{\Phi,n^{\prime}+2}}\lambda\mu^{n^{\prime}+2}.

(5.14)

Finally, to prove (
5.10
), we use both
e^{-i\zeta z}=iz^{-1}\partial_{\zeta}e^{-i\zeta z}
and
e^{-i\zeta z}=i\zeta^{-1}\partial_{z}e^{-i\zeta z}
and integration by parts to estimate

\displaystyle\left|{Q_{Z,H}}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|{\iint\partial_{\zeta}^{m}\left(\chi_{H}(\zeta)\widetilde{q}(x,\xi;z,\zeta)\right)\chi_{Z}(z)e^{ir^{(3)}[\Phi](x+z,x)\frac{z^{3}}{3!}}z^{-m}e^{-i\zeta z}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta\mathrm{d}z}\right|

\displaystyle=\left|{\iint\partial_{z}^{n}\left(\partial_{\zeta}^{m}\left(\chi_{H}(\zeta)\widetilde{q}(x,\xi;z,\zeta)\right)\chi_{Z}(z)z^{-m}e^{ir^{(3)}[\Phi](x+z,x)\frac{z^{3}}{3!}}\right)\zeta^{-n}e^{-i\zeta z}\,\mathrm{d}\zeta\mathrm{d}z}\right|.

From the last line, the desired estimate (
5.10
) follows, via the chain rule and the Leibniz rule, from (
5.9
) and (
5.14
).

We are now ready to prove (
5.6
). In what follows, we omit the dependence of implicit constants on
A_{\Phi,10C_{0}}
and
A_{q,10C_{0}}
. Consider (
5.10
) with
m=C_{0}+2
and
n=3m+C_{0}+2
, and sum over
Z\gtrsim\lambda_{q}^{-1}
,
H\gtrsim\lambda_{q}
. Then

\displaystyle\sum_{Z\gtrsim\lambda_{q}^{-1},\,H\gtrsim\lambda_{q}}|{Q_{Z,H}(x,\xi)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim g(x)\sum_{Z\gtrsim\lambda_{q}^{-1},\,H\gtrsim\lambda_{q}}M_{Z,H}\cdot H^{-m}\max\left\{Z^{-1},\frac{\lambda\mu}{\lambda_{q}},\mu,\lambda\mu^{2}Z^{2}+\lambda\mu^{3}Z^{3}\right\}^{m}Z^{-n}\lambda_{q}^{-n}

\displaystyle\lesssim g(x)\sum_{H\gtrsim\lambda_{q},}\left(\sum_{Z\gtrsim\lambda_{q}^{-1}}M_{Z,H}(Z\lambda_{q})^{-C_{0}}\right)\,H^{-m}\max\left\{\lambda_{q},\frac{\lambda\mu}{\lambda_{q}},\mu,\lambda\mu^{2}\lambda_{q}^{-2}+\lambda\mu^{3}\lambda_{q}^{-3}\right\}^{m}

\displaystyle\lesssim g(x)\left(\sum_{Z\gtrsim\lambda_{q}^{-1},\,H\gtrsim\lambda_{q}}M_{Z,H}(Z\lambda_{q})^{-C_{0}}(H\lambda_{q}^{-1})^{-C_{0}}\right)\max\left\{1,\mu\lambda_{q}^{-1},\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{q}}\left(\mu\lambda_{q}^{-1}+\mu^{3}\lambda_{q}^{-3}\right)\right\}^{C_{0}+2}

\displaystyle\lesssim g(x)\mathcal{M}_{<\lambda_{q}^{-1},<\lambda_{q}}\max\left\{1,\mu\lambda_{q}^{-1},\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{q}}\left(\mu\lambda_{q}^{-1}+\mu^{3}\lambda_{q}^{-3}\right)\right\}^{C_{0}+2}.

Next, invoking (
5.11
) with
m=C_{0}+2
and summing over
H\gtrsim\lambda_{q}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{H\gtrsim\lambda_{q}}|{Q_{<\lambda_{q}^{-1},H}(x,\xi)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim g(x)\sum_{H\gtrsim\lambda_{q}}M_{<\lambda_{q}^{-1},H}\cdot H^{-m}\max\left\{\lambda_{q},\frac{\lambda\mu}{\lambda_{q}},\mu,\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{q}}\frac{\mu^{2}}{\lambda_{q}}+\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{q}}\frac{\mu^{3}}{\lambda_{q}^{2}}\right\}^{m}

\displaystyle\lesssim g(x)\left(\sum_{H\gtrsim\lambda_{q}}M_{<\lambda_{q}^{-1},H}(H\lambda_{q}^{-1})^{-C_{0}}\right)\max\left\{1,\mu\lambda_{q}^{-1},\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{q}}\left(\mu\lambda_{q}^{-1}+\mu^{3}\lambda_{q}^{-3}\right)\right\}^{C_{0}+2}

\displaystyle\lesssim g(x)\mathcal{M}_{<\lambda_{q}^{-1},<\lambda_{q}}\max\left\{1,\mu\lambda_{q}^{-1},\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{q}}\left(\mu\lambda_{q}^{-1}+\mu^{3}\lambda_{q}^{-3}\right)\right\}^{C_{0}+2}.

From (
5.12
) with
n=C_{0}+2
and summing over
Z\gtrsim\lambda_{q}^{-1}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{Z\gtrsim\lambda_{q}^{-1}}|{Q_{Z,<\lambda_{q}}(x,\xi)}|

\displaystyle\lesssim g(x)\sum_{Z\gtrsim\lambda_{q}^{-1}}M_{Z,<\lambda_{q}}\left(Z^{-1}\lambda_{q}^{-1}\right)^{n}

\displaystyle\lesssim g(x)\left(\sum_{Z\gtrsim\lambda_{q}^{-1}}M_{Z,<\lambda_{q}}(Z\lambda_{q})^{-C_{0}}\right)

\displaystyle\lesssim g(x)\mathcal{M}_{<\lambda_{q}^{-1},<\lambda_{q}}.

Combining the preceding three bounds with (
5.13
), we obtain (
5.6
) with
\ell=0
.

To handle the case
\ell>0
, note in (
5.8
) that the
\xi
-dependence of
Q(x,\xi)
arises entirely from that of
\widetilde{q}(x,\xi;z,\zeta)
in the integrand. By (
5.7
) and the definition of
\widetilde{q}(x,\xi;z,\zeta)
, observe that

|{\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}\partial_{\zeta}^{m}\partial_{z}^{n}\widetilde{q}(x,\xi;z,\zeta)}|\lesssim_{\ell,m,n,A_{\Phi,n},A_{q,\ell+m+n}}\lambda_{q}^{-\ell}\times(\hbox{RHS of \eqref{eq:Q-ampl-reg}}).

Using the preceding bound in place of (
5.9
) and repeating the remainder of the
\ell=0
proof, we obtain (
5.6
) with higher
\ell
’s.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

Under additional assumptions on
x
-derivatives of
q(x_{1},x,\lambda_{0},\xi)
, the computation as above also yields bounds on
\partial_{x}^{k}Q
. For instance, if the symbol
q
is independent of
x
, then
|{\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}Q}|\lesssim\left(\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{q}}\mu\max\{1,\mu^{2}\lambda_{q}^{-2}\}\right)^{k}\times(\hbox{RHS of \eqref{eq:osc-Q-key} with $g\equiv 1$})
. In this case, by the Calderón–Vaillancourt theorem,
Q(x,D)
is
L^{2}
-bounded with norm
O(\mathcal{M}_{<\lambda_{q},<\lambda_{q}^{-1}})
as long as
\frac{\lambda\mu}{\lambda_{q}^{2}}=O(1)
and
\lambda_{q}^{-1}\mu=O(1)
. However, since we are able to exploit higher derivatives bounds for
a
in (
5.5
) (which originate from Section
4
), such spatial derivative bounds for
Q
will not be necessary; see Section
5.2
below.

5.2
Kernel bounds and proof of Proposition
5.1

To complete the proof of Proposition
5.1
, it remains to translate the symbol bound (
5.6
) to an operator bound for
{}^{(\Phi)}q(x_{2},D_{2};\sigma)
. As we are allowed to use high derivatives of
a
on the RHS of (
5.5
), it suffices to utilize the following simple kernel bound:

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
p(x,\xi)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}\times\mathbb{R})
satisfy, for any
0\leq\ell\leq n
,

|{\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}p(x,\xi)}|\leq A_{p,\ell}\lambda_{p}^{-\ell}G(x,\xi),

(5.15)

where
A_{p,\ell}
are increasing.
Let
\nu\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}}
and
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then the kernel
K_{p(-\Delta)^{-k}P_{\nu}}(x,y)
of the operator
p(x,D)(-\Delta)^{-k}P_{\nu}(D)u

obeys, for any
x\neq y
, the pointwise bound

|{K_{p(-\Delta)^{-k}P_{\nu}}(x,y)}|\lesssim_{n,A_{p,n},k}\frac{\nu^{-2k}\int_{\frac{1}{4}\nu<|{\xi}|<4\nu}G(x,\xi)\,\mathrm{d}\xi}{\langle{\min\{\nu,\lambda_{p}\}(x-y)}\rangle^{n}}.

(5.16)

Moreover, the kernel
K_{pP_{<\nu}}(x,y)
of the operator
p(x,D)P_{<\nu}(D)u
obeys the same bound as
K_{pP_{\nu}}
.

Proof.

By definition,

p(x,D)P_{\nu}(D)u=\int p(x,\xi)P_{\nu}(\xi)\hat{u}(\xi)e^{ix\cdot\xi}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}\xi}{2\pi}=\int K_{pP_{\nu}}(x,y)u(y)\,\mathrm{d}y,

so formally,

K_{p(-\Delta)^{-2k}P_{\nu}}(x,y)=\int p(x,\xi)|{\xi}|^{-2k}P_{\nu}(\xi)e^{i(x-y)\cdot\xi}\,\frac{\mathrm{d}\xi}{2\pi}.

Thanks to the compact support property of
P_{\nu}(\xi)
, this formal computation is readily justified. Moreover, by the identity
e^{i(x-y)\cdot\xi}=(i(x_{j}-y_{j}))^{-1}\partial_{\xi_{j}}e^{i(x-y)\cdot\xi}
, integration by parts and using the bounds (
5.15
) and
|{\partial_{\xi}^{\ell}P_{\nu}(\xi)}|\lesssim_{\ell}\nu^{-\ell}
, (
5.16
) follows. The case of
K_{pP_{<\nu}}
is entirely analogous. ∎

We are now ready to complete the proof of Proposition
5.1
.

Proof.

We introduce the shorthand
Q(x_{2},\xi_{2})={}^{(\Phi)}q(x_{2},D_{2};\sigma)
. We begin by splitting

\displaystyle Q(x_{2},D_{2})a=Q(x_{2},D_{2})P_{<\mu}(D_{2})a+\sum_{\nu\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}},\nu>\mu}{}^{(\Phi)}q(x_{2},D_{2};\sigma)P_{\nu}(D_{2})a.

Consider the summand
Q(x_{2},D_{2})P_{\nu}(D_{2})a
with
\nu>\mu
. By Lemma
5.3
with
C_{0}=\frac{N}{100}
and (
5.4
),
Q(x_{2},\xi_{2})
obeys (
5.15
) with
G(x,\xi)=g(x_{2})\mathcal{M}_{<\lambda_{q}^{-1},<\lambda_{q}}(x_{2},\xi_{2};\sigma)
. By Lemma
5.5
with
n=N
, the kernel of
Q(x_{2},D_{2})P_{\nu}(D_{2})
obeys the bound

\displaystyle|{K_{Q(-\partial_{x_{2}}^{2})^{-k}P_{\nu}}(x_{2},y_{2})}|

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\nu^{-2k}g(x_{2})\int_{|{\xi_{2}}|\simeq\nu}\mathcal{M}_{<\lambda_{q}^{-1},<\lambda_{q}}(x_{2},\xi_{2};\sigma)\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}}{\langle{\min\{\nu,\lambda_{q}\}(x_{2}-y_{2})}\rangle^{N}}.

Note that, for
\nu\geq\mu
,

\displaystyle\frac{g(x_{2})}{\langle{\nu(y_{2}-x_{2})}\rangle^{\frac{N}{100}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{g(x_{2})}{\langle{\mu(y_{2}-x_{2})}\rangle^{\frac{N}{100}}}\lesssim\sup_{z_{2}}\frac{g(z_{2})}{\langle{\mu(y_{2}-z_{2})}\rangle^{C_{1}}}=\overline{g}_{<\mu^{-1}}(y_{2}),

\displaystyle\frac{\int_{|{\xi_{2}}|\simeq\nu}\mathcal{M}_{<\lambda_{q}^{-1},<\lambda_{q}}(x_{2},\xi_{2};\sigma)\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}}{\langle{\nu(y_{2}-x_{2})}\rangle^{\frac{N}{100}}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\int_{|{\xi_{2}}|\simeq\nu}\mathcal{M}_{<\lambda_{q}^{-1},<\lambda_{q}}(x_{2},\xi_{2};\sigma)\frac{\mu^{-1}}{\langle{\mu^{-1}\xi_{2}}\rangle^{\frac{N}{100}}}\mathrm{d}\xi_{2}}{\langle{\mu(y_{2}-x_{2})}\rangle^{\frac{N}{100}}}\mu\left(\frac{\nu}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{N}{100}}

\displaystyle\leq\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{<\mu^{-1}}(x_{2};\sigma)\mu\left(\frac{\nu}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{N}{100}},

\displaystyle{\frac{\langle{\mu(x_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}}{\langle{\nu(y_{2}-x_{2})}\rangle^{|{s}|}}}

\displaystyle{\leq\frac{\langle{\mu(x_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}}{\langle{\mu(y_{2}-x_{2})}\rangle^{|{s}|}}\lesssim\langle{\mu(y_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}.}

Therefore,

\displaystyle|{K_{Q(-\partial_{x_{2}}^{2})^{-k}P_{\nu}}(x_{2},y_{2})}|{\langle{\mu(x_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{-2k}\left(\frac{\mu}{\min\{\nu,\lambda_{q}\}}\right)\left(\frac{\nu}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{N}{100}}\overline{g}_{<\mu^{-1}}(y_{2})\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{<\mu^{-1}}(y_{2};\sigma){\langle{\mu(y_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}}\frac{\min\{\nu,\lambda_{q}\}}{\langle{\min\{\nu,\lambda_{q}\}(x_{2}-y_{2})}\rangle^{\frac{N}{2}}},

Since
\frac{N}{2}>1
, the last factor defines a kernel that is
L^{2}
-bounded. Hence,

\displaystyle\|{Q(x_{2},D_{2})P_{\nu}(D_{2})a{\langle{\mu(x_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\nu^{-2k}\left(\frac{\mu}{\min\{\nu,\lambda_{q}\}}\right)\left(\frac{\nu}{\mu}\right)^{\frac{N}{100}}\|{\overline{g}_{<\mu^{-1}}(y_{2})\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{<\mu^{-1}}(y_{2};\sigma)\partial_{x_{2}}^{2k}a(y_{2}){\langle{\mu(y_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}}}\|_{L^{2}_{y_{2}}}

Choosing
k=\lfloor\frac{N}{2}\rfloor
and summing over all dyadic numbers
\nu>\mu
(here, we use
N\gg 100
), we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu\in 2^{\mathbb{Z}},\,\nu>\mu}\|{Q(x_{2},D_{2})P_{\nu}(D_{2})a{\langle{\mu(x_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\overline{g}_{<\mu^{-1}}(y_{2})\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{<\mu^{-1}}(y_{2};\sigma)(\mu^{-1}\partial_{x_{2}})^{2\lfloor\frac{N}{2}\rfloor}a(y_{2})\langle{\mu(y_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}}\|_{L^{2}_{y_{2}}},

which is acceptable. Moreover, the following can be proved in an entirely analogous manner:

\displaystyle\|{Q(x_{2},D_{2})P_{<\mu}(D_{2})a{\langle{\mu(x_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|{\overline{g}_{<\mu^{-1}}(y_{2})\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{<\mu^{-1}}(y_{2};\sigma)a(y_{2}){\langle{\mu(y_{2}-X_{0})}\rangle^{s}}}\|_{L^{2}_{y_{2}}}.

Combining the last two bounds, we obtain (
5.5
). ∎

6
Construction of degenerating wave packets

In this section, we finally put together all tools developed so far to construct degenerating wave packets for
\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}
.

6.1
Specification of the construction

As in Section
4
, we are given: a symbol
\gamma
and a function
f
that satisfy the assumptions in Section
1.5
;
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
,
M>1
and
\delta_{0}>0
that satisfy (
1.18
)–(
1.20
). When
f
is time-dependent, we are given
\delta_{1}>0
such that (
1.25
) hold, as well as that
f
is even. To apply the results of Section
4
, we need to further specify
\delta_{2}>0
and
N_{0}
;
\delta_{2}
will be specified below in Proposition
6.3
, while
N_{0}
will be chosen in Section
7
(see Proposition
6.3
for the condition that
N_{0}
has to satisfy).

We apply the results of Section
4
with the above parameters, and obtain a global phase function
\Phi=\Phi(t,x_{2})
on
[0,\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})]\times\mathbb{R}
as in Section
4.6
, which agrees with the solution to the Hamilton–Jacobi equation (
4.1
) constructed in Sections
4.2
–
4.3
in the region
X(t,x^{\prime}_{0})\leq x\leq X(t,x^{\prime}_{1})
. We inherit the parameters
\delta_{3}
,
\delta_{4}
,
\delta_{5}
and
T
fixed in Section
4
(see Section
4.1
). The parameters
c_{x_{0}}
and
\Lambda
, which were not fixed in Section
4
, will be chosen in this section; we shall fix
c_{x_{0}}
in this subsection, and
\Lambda\geq 1
in Section
6.2
.

Given the global phase function
\Phi
from Section
4
, the degenerating wave packet takes the form

\widetilde{\varphi}(t,x_{1},x_{2})=\Re\left(e^{i\lambda_{0}x_{1}}\widetilde{\psi}\right),\quad\hbox{ where }\widetilde{\psi}=a(t,x_{2})e^{i\Phi(t,x_{2})},

(6.1)

and
a
solves (
3.16
). When
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
,
we fix a choice of
c_{x_{0}}
so that it obeys all the requirements in Section
4
, then we take as the initial data for
a
the following:

a_{0}(x_{2})=\frac{1}{(x^{\prime}_{1}-x^{\prime}_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}\chi\left(\frac{x_{2}-x^{\prime}_{0}}{x^{\prime}_{1}-x^{\prime}_{0}}\right),

(6.2)

where
\chi(\cdot)
is a smooth nonnegative function supported in
[0,1]
, so that
\supp a_{0}\subseteq(x_{0},x_{1})
and
\|{a_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}=1
. Then
\supp a(t,\cdot)\subseteq(X(t;x^{\prime}_{0}),X(t;x^{\prime}_{1}))
, so that
\Phi(t,x_{2})
solves (
3.15
) in the support of
\widetilde{\varphi}
; hence, the computation in Section
3
is applicable. Moreover, recalling (
4.11
) or (
4.27
) and (
4.76
), as well as the fact that
x^{\prime}_{0}\simeq x_{0}
and
x^{\prime}_{1}-x^{\prime}_{0}\simeq\Delta x_{0}
, we have, for any
k\geq 0
,

\|{(\mu_{0}^{-1}\partial_{x})^{k}a_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{k}1,

so that Proposition
4.12
is useful. Moreover,
\|{\widetilde{\varphi}(0,x_{1},x_{2})}\|_{L^{2}_{x_{1},x_{2}}(\Omega)}=1
, and for
m_{0}>0
sufficiently large,

\|{P_{m_{0}^{-1}\lambda_{0}<\cdot<m_{0}\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})(e^{i\Phi(0,x_{2})}a_{0})}\|_{L^{2}_{x_{2}}}\gtrsim 1,

where the implicit constant is independent of
\lambda_{0}
.

When
\Omega=\mathbb{T}^{2}
, we simply periodize (
6.1
) and set

\widetilde{\varphi}(t,x_{1},x_{2})=\sum_{n\in\mathbb{Z}}{}^{(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R})}\widetilde{\varphi}(t,x_{1},x_{2}-n).

(6.3)

where
{}^{(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R})}\widetilde{\varphi}
is the wave packet in the case
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
.
Choosing
c_{x_{0}}>0
to be sufficiently small
, we may ensure that
each summand (in particular,
a_{0}
) is supported in a fundamental domain
.

6.2
Degeneration and initial estimates for the wave packet

The purpose of this subsection is to obtain sharp bounds on the
H^{s}
-norm of
\widetilde{\varphi}
for a suitable range of
s
. In what follows, the following convention is in effect: If
\|{\cdot}\|_{X_{x_{2}}}
is a norm for functions of
x_{2}
, then
\|{\cdot}\|_{X_{x_{2}}(\Omega)}
denotes either
\|{\cdot}\|_{X_{x_{2}}(\mathbb{R})}
or
\|{\cdot}\|_{X_{x_{2}}(\mathbb{T})}
depending on whether
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, respectively
. We also introduce, for any
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
L>0
,

\|{a}\|_{H_{(L)}^{k}(\Omega)}^{2}=\sum_{k^{\prime}=0}^{k}\|{(L\partial_{x_{2}})^{k^{\prime}}a}\|_{L^{2}_{x_{2}}(\Omega)}^{2}.

Proposition 6.1

(Degeneration and initial estimates)
.

Let
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{T}^{2}
, and let
\widetilde{\varphi}
be given as in Section
6.1
. For
\lambda_{0}\geq\Lambda
sufficiently large (depending on
\gamma
and the parameters fixed in Section
4
), the following statements hold.

1.

There exists a decomposition

\widetilde{\varphi}=\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}+\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}

such that for any
s,\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
and
N\in\mathbb{N}
such that
|{s}|+\beta_{0}|{\sigma}|\leq N-1
and
0\leq t\leq\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})
,

\displaystyle\|{\Gamma^{\sigma}\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}(t,x_{1},x_{2})}\|_{H^{s}_{x_{2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma,f,N}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda)^{\sigma}\lambda^{s}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}^{N}(\Omega)},

(6.4)

\displaystyle\|{\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}(t,x_{1},x_{2})}\|_{L^{2}_{x_{2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma,f}\lambda^{-1}\mu\|{a_{0}}\|_{H_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}^{1}(\Omega)}.

(6.5)

2.

At
t=0
, we additionally have, for any
m_{0}>0
(and taking
\Lambda
larger if necessary),

\displaystyle\|{\Gamma^{\sigma}\widetilde{\varphi}(0,x_{1},x_{2})}\|_{H^{s}_{x_{2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma,f,N}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda_{0})^{\sigma}\lambda_{0}^{s}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}^{N}(\Omega)},

(6.6)

\displaystyle\|{\Gamma^{\sigma}\widetilde{\varphi}(0,x_{1},x_{2})}\|_{H^{s}_{x_{2}}(\Omega)}

\displaystyle\gtrsim_{\gamma,f,m_{0}}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda_{0})^{\sigma}\lambda_{0}^{s}\|{P_{m_{0}^{-1}\lambda_{0}<\cdot<m_{0}\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})(e^{i\Phi(0,x_{2})}a_{0}(x_{2}))}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)},

(6.7)

Moreover, analogous conclusions hold for
\widetilde{\psi}
.

The core ingredient of the proof of Proposition
6.1
is the following simple lemma (on
\mathbb{R}
):

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\lambda\leq|{\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi(t,x_{2})}|\leq\overline{\lambda}
and
|{\partial_{x_{2}}^{\ell}\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi}|\leq A_{\Phi,\ell}\mu^{\ell}\overline{\lambda}
on
\mathbb{R}
for
1\leq\ell\leq n
, where
A_{\Phi,\ell}>0
is increasing. Then for any
\nu\geq 1
and function
a=a(x_{2})
,

\displaystyle\|{P_{\nu}(D_{2})(e^{i\Phi}a)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,A_{\Phi,n}}\min\left\{\left(\frac{\nu+(\overline{\lambda}/\lambda)\mu}{\lambda}\right)^{n},\left(\frac{\overline{\lambda}+\mu}{\nu}\right)^{n}\right\}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}\|{(\mu^{-1}\partial_{x_{2}})^{\ell}a}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})},

(6.8)

\displaystyle\|{P_{<\nu}(D_{2})(e^{i\Phi}a)}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{n,A_{\Phi,n}}\left(\frac{\nu+(\overline{\lambda}/\lambda)\mu}{\lambda}\right)^{n}\sum_{\ell=0}^{n}\|{(\mu^{-1}\partial_{x_{2}})^{\ell}a}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{R})}.

(6.9)

Proof.

To prove (
6.8
) for
n=1
, we write

\displaystyle P_{\nu}(D_{2})\left(ae^{i\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle=P_{\nu}(D_{2})\left(\tfrac{a}{i\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi}\partial_{x_{2}}e^{i\Phi}\right)=-P_{\nu}(D_{2})D_{2}\left(\tfrac{a}{\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi}e^{i\Phi}\right)-P_{\nu}\left(\partial_{x_{2}}(\tfrac{a}{i\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi})e^{i\Phi}\right),\hbox{ or }

\displaystyle P_{\nu}(D_{2})\left(ae^{i\Phi}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{P_{\nu}(D_{2})D_{2}}{|{D_{2}}|^{2}}i\partial_{x_{2}}\left(ae^{i\Phi}\right),

and use the hypotheses, as well as the bounds
\|{P_{\nu}(D_{2})D_{2}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim\nu
and
\|{\tfrac{P_{\nu}(D_{2})D_{2}}{|{D_{2}}|^{2}}}\|_{L^{2}\to L^{2}}\lesssim\nu^{-1}
. Higher
n
’s are handled by iteration. Finally, (
6.9
) is proved using an analogous identity as the first one for
P_{<\nu}
. ∎

Proof of Proposition
6.1
.

To begin with, observe that, thanks to the support conditions for
\widetilde{\varphi}
and
a_{0}
, we may assume that
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
without any loss of generality. Note the identity

\langle{D}\rangle^{s}\Gamma^{\sigma}\widetilde{\varphi}=\langle{D}\rangle^{s}\Gamma^{\sigma}\Re(e^{i\lambda_{0}x_{1}}\widetilde{\psi})=\tfrac{1}{2}(e^{i\lambda_{0}x_{1}}\langle{D_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}^{s}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})^{\sigma}\widetilde{\psi}+e^{-i\lambda_{0}x_{1}}\langle{D_{2}}\rangle_{-\lambda_{0}}^{s}\gamma_{-\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})^{\sigma}\overline{\widetilde{\psi}}),

(6.10)

where
\langle{\xi_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}^{s}=(\lambda_{0}^{2}+\xi_{2}^{2})^{\frac{s}{2}}
. In view of the symmetries
\langle{\xi_{2}}\rangle_{-\lambda_{0}}=\langle{\xi_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}
and
\gamma_{-\lambda_{0}}(\xi_{2})=\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(-\xi_{2})
, the two terms on the RHS are always treated similarly. In what follows, we focus on
\langle{D_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}^{s}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})^{\sigma}\widetilde{\psi}
, except in the proof of (
6.7
).
To simplify the notation, we shall abbreviate
P_{\nu}=P_{\nu}(D_{2})
,
L^{2}=L^{2}_{x_{2}}
and
H^{s}=H^{s}_{x_{2}}
in this proof. We shall also often suppress the dependence of constants on
\gamma
and
f
.

Note that
\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi
obeys the hypothesis of Lemma
6.2
with
\overline{\lambda}\lesssim\lambda
. Applying Lemma
6.2
for any
0\leq n\leq N
, then using Proposition
4.12
to bound
\|{a}\|_{H_{(\mu^{-1})}^{n}}\lesssim\|{a_{0}}\|_{H_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}^{n}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\|{P_{\nu}(D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\min\left\{\left(\frac{\nu+\mu}{\lambda}\right)^{n},\left(\frac{\lambda+\mu}{\nu}\right)^{n}\right\}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{n}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}},

(6.11)

\displaystyle\|{P_{<\nu}(D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\left(\frac{\nu+\mu}{\lambda}\right)^{n}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{n}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}}.

(6.12)

From (
4.48
), note moreover that

\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\lesssim\lambda_{0}^{-1+2\delta_{3}N_{0}}(\log\lambda_{0})^{-2}\epsilon(\lambda_{0})^{-1}x_{0}^{-1}=\lambda_{0}^{-1+2\delta_{3}N_{0}}(\log\lambda_{0})^{-2}\epsilon(\lambda_{0})^{-2}c_{x_{0}}^{-1},

(6.13)

where the implicit constant depends only on
\gamma
. Recall (
4.3
) and that
\delta_{3}\ll\frac{\delta_{2}}{N_{0}}\ll\frac{\delta_{0}}{N_{0}}
(see Proposition
4.10
). Hence, by taking
\lambda_{0}\geq\Lambda
sufficiently large, we may ensure that

\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\leq\lambda_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{1}{10}\delta_{0}}.

(6.14)

We also observe that, in view of Assumption 1 for
\Gamma
, we have, for any
b\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R})
and
\nu>0
,

\displaystyle\|{\langle{D_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})P_{\nu}b}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma,s,\sigma}(\nu+\lambda_{0})^{s}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\nu)^{\sigma}\|{P_{\nu}b}\|_{L^{2}},

(6.15)

\displaystyle\|{\langle{D_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})P_{<\lambda_{0}}b}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{\gamma,s,\sigma}\lambda_{0}^{s}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda_{0})^{\sigma}\|{P_{<\lambda_{0}}b}\|_{L^{2}}.

(6.16)

Now we are ready to begin the proof of the proposition in earnest. To prove the first statement, we begin by defining

\widetilde{\psi}^{main}=\sum_{\nu\geq\mu}P_{\nu}\widetilde{\psi},\qquad\widetilde{\psi}^{small}=\widetilde{\psi}-\widetilde{\psi}^{main},

and, accordingly,
\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}=\Re(e^{i\lambda_{0}x_{1}}\widetilde{\psi}^{main})
and
\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}=\widetilde{\varphi}-\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}
.
Applying (
6.11
) with
n=N
, as well as (
6.15
) and (
1.11
), we obtain

\displaystyle\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda)^{-\sigma}\lambda^{-s}\|{\langle{D_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})\sum_{\nu\geq\mu}P_{\nu}\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim_{N}\sum_{\mu\leq\nu<\max\{\mu,\lambda_{0}\}}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\lambda_{0}}\right)^{\beta_{0}|{\sigma}|}\left(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\lambda}\right)^{s}\left(\frac{\nu}{\lambda}\right)^{N}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{N}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+\sum_{\max\{\mu,\lambda_{0}\}\leq\nu<\lambda}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\nu}\right)^{\beta_{0}|{\sigma}|}\left(\frac{\nu}{\lambda}\right)^{s}\left(\frac{\nu}{\lambda}\right)^{N}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{N}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+\sum_{\nu>\lambda}\left(\frac{\nu}{\lambda}\right)^{\beta_{0}|{\sigma}|}\left(\frac{\nu}{\lambda}\right)^{s}\left(\frac{\lambda}{\nu}\right)^{N}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{N}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}}.

By the assumed lower bound on
N
, the RHS may be bounded by
\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{N}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}}
(up to a constant), which implies (
6.4
). On the other hand, for
\widetilde{\psi}^{small}=P_{<\mu}\widetilde{\psi}
, (
6.5
) follows quickly from (
6.12
) with
n=1
and Proposition
4.12
.

Next, we turn to the second statement. Note that
(\lambda,\mu,a)=(\lambda_{0},\mu_{0},a_{0})
at
t=0
. In order to establish (
6.6
), in view of (
6.4
), it suffices to prove

\|{\langle{D_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}\Gamma_{\lambda_{0}}^{\sigma}(D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}^{small}(t=0)}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda_{0})^{\sigma}\lambda_{0}^{s}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}^{1}}.

Since
\widetilde{\psi}^{small}(t=0)=P_{<\mu_{0}}\widetilde{\psi}_{0}
and
\mu_{0}<\lambda_{0}
, the desired bound follows from (
6.12
) and (
6.16
).
To prove (
6.7
), observe that, by (
6.10
) and orthogonality in the frequency space (first in
\xi_{1}
and then in
\xi_{2}
), we have

\displaystyle\|{\langle{D}\rangle^{s}\Gamma^{\sigma}\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}_{x_{1},x_{2}}}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}\|{e^{i\lambda_{0}x_{1}}\langle{D_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}^{s}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})^{\sigma}\widetilde{\psi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}_{x_{1},x_{2}}}\gtrsim\|{\langle{D_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}^{s}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})^{\sigma}P_{m_{0}^{-1}\lambda_{0}<\cdot<m_{0}\lambda_{0}}\widetilde{\psi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}.

Hence, it suffices to prove

\displaystyle\|{P_{m_{0}^{-1}\lambda_{0}<\cdot<m_{0}\lambda_{0}}\widetilde{\psi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim_{m_{0}}\lambda_{0}^{-s}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda_{0})^{-\sigma}\|{\langle{D_{2}}\rangle_{\lambda_{0}}^{s}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(D_{2})^{\sigma}P_{m_{0}^{-1}\lambda_{0}<\cdot<m_{0}\lambda_{0}}\widetilde{\psi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}},

which follows from (
6.15
). This completes the proof. ∎

We may now fix our choice of
\Lambda
so that it obeys all the requirements that appeared so far.

6.3
Error estimate

The main result of this subsection is the following:

Proposition 6.3

(Error estimate)
.

Let
\widetilde{\varphi}
be constructed as in Section
6.1
, with
\delta_{2}=\delta_{0}^{10}
and
N_{0}\geq\frac{10^{4}}{\delta_{2}}(1+\beta_{0})
. Then there exists
\delta_{6}>0
such that

\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})}\|{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}_{x_{1},x_{2}}(\Omega)}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\lambda_{0}^{-\delta_{6}}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{N_{0}}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}(\Omega)}.

(6.17)

Proof.

By translation in
x_{2}
and rescaling
t
, we assume, without loss of generality, that
\mathring{x}_{2}=0
and
\partial_{x_{2}}^{2}f(0,0)=-1
. We let all implicit constants in this proof to depend on
\gamma
and
f
. Note that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}

\displaystyle=\Re\left(\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}(e^{i\lambda_{0}x_{1}}\widetilde{\psi})\right)

\displaystyle=\Re\left(e^{i\lambda_{0}x_{1}}\left(ip_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})+s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})+r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})\right)\widetilde{\psi}\right).

Hence, in what follows, we focus on estimating the
L^{1}_{t}L^{2}_{x_{2}}
-norm of

\left(ip_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})+s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})+r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})\right)\widetilde{\psi}.

We first treat the case
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
.

Step 1: Contribution of
ip_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}+s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}
, low frequency input.

By Assumption 1 on
\gamma
, it follows that

\displaystyle\left|{\partial_{x_{2}}^{k}\partial_{\xi_{2}}^{\ell}\left(\left(ip_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},\xi_{2})+s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},\xi_{2})\right)P_{<\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}(\xi_{2})\right)}\right|\lesssim_{k,\ell}\langle{\xi_{2}}\rangle^{-\ell}\lambda_{0}\gamma(\lambda_{0},\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}).

By the standard
L^{2}
-boundedness of a pseudo-differential operator with a classical symbol and Lemma
6.2
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\left(ip_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})+s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})\right)P_{<\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}(D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}}\right\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lambda_{0}(\max\{\lambda_{0},\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}\})^{\beta_{0}}\left(\frac{\mu+\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}{\lambda}\right)^{N}\|{a}\|_{H_{(\mu^{-1})}^{N}}.

By (
6.14
), we see that
\frac{\mu+\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}{\lambda}\leq\max\{\lambda_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{1}{10}\delta_{0}},\lambda^{-\delta_{2}}\}
. Choosing
N=\delta_{2}^{-1}(\beta_{0}+2)
, which bounded by
N_{0}
, then applying Proposition
4.12
, the RHS is
O(\lambda_{0}^{-1}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{N_{0}}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}})
, which is sufficient.

Step 2: Decomposition of
ip_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}+s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}

Thanks to our pointwise bound
\lambda\leq\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi\leq 2\lambda
, observe that

\displaystyle p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi)P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}(\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi)

\displaystyle=p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi),

\displaystyle s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi)P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}(\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi)

\displaystyle=s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},\partial_{x_{2}}\Phi),

so that by definition,

{}^{(\Phi)}r_{p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}},-2}={}^{(\Phi)}r_{p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}},-2},\quad{}^{(\Phi)}r_{s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}},-1}={}^{(\Phi)}r_{s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}},-1}.

To proceed, we furthermore split
p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}
into
p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{main}+p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{rem}

\displaystyle p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{main}(x_{2},\xi_{2})

\displaystyle=\partial_{x_{2}}f(t,x_{2})\lambda_{0}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\xi_{2}),

\displaystyle p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{rem}(x_{2},\xi_{2})

\displaystyle=\Gamma\partial_{x_{2}}f(t,x_{2})\lambda_{0}.

By the linear dependence of
{}^{(\Phi)}r_{p,-k}
on
p
, the remaining task reduces to estimating

\displaystyle\left(i{}^{(\Phi)}r_{p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{main}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}},-2}+i{}^{(\Phi)}r_{p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{rem}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}},-2}+{}^{(\Phi)}r_{s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}},-1}\right)(x_{2},D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}.

Step 3: Contribution of
ip_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{main}
.

In this step, we estimate the contribution of
p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{main}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}
. Observe that this symbol obeys

\partial_{\xi_{2}}^{\ell}\left(p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{main}(x_{2},\xi_{2})P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}(\xi_{2})\right)\lesssim_{\ell}\lambda^{-\ell(1-\delta_{2})}|{\partial_{x_{2}}f(x_{2})}|\lambda_{0}m(\lambda_{0},\xi_{2}).

As a result, each term in (
3.8
) for
p=p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{main}(x_{2},\xi_{2})P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}(\xi_{2})
is of the form (after rewriting
\xi_{2}=\mu\cdot(\xi_{2}/\mu)
)

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{1}{}^{(\Phi)}q(x_{2},\xi_{2};\sigma)\,\mathrm{d}\sigma\left(\frac{\xi_{2}}{\mu}\right)^{\ell},\qquad\ell=0,1,2,\end{split}

where
q
obeys the hypothesis of Proposition
5.1
with

\lambda_{q}=\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}},\quad g(x_{2})=|{f^{\prime}(x_{2})}|,\quad M(\xi_{2})=\lambda_{0}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\xi_{2})\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{{\lambda^{2(1-\delta_{2})}}}+\frac{\mu^{2}\lambda}{{\lambda^{3(1-\delta_{2})}}}+\frac{\mu^{2}\lambda^{2}}{{\lambda^{4(1-\delta_{2})}}}\right).

In order to apply Proposition
5.1
, note that, since
\frac{\mu}{\lambda_{q}}\lesssim\lambda_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{1}{20}\delta_{0}}
by (
6.14
),
f^{\prime}
is smooth and bounded and
\gamma
obeys (
1.11
), we have

\overline{g}_{<\mu^{-1}}\lesssim x_{2}+\mu^{-1},\qquad\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{<\mu^{-1}}\lesssim M(\lambda),

where we choose
N=1000(1+\beta_{0})
. We remark that the second inequality may be readily verified using the fact that
M(\xi_{2})
is increasing and slowly varying (i.e., Assumptions 1 and 2 for
\Gamma
). Putting together the above observations with Propositions
3.3
and
5.1
(with
s=0
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\|{{}^{(\Phi)}r_{p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{main}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}},-2}\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(X(t;x_{0})+\mu^{-1})\lambda_{0}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda)\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{{\lambda^{2(1-\delta_{2})}}}+\frac{\mu^{2}\lambda}{{\lambda^{3(1-\delta_{2})}}}+\frac{\mu^{2}\lambda^{2}}{{\lambda^{4(1-\delta_{2})}}}\right)\|{a}\|_{H_{(\mu^{-1})}^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim(X(t;x_{0})+\mu^{-1})\lambda_{0}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda)\left(\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\lambda_{0}^{\frac{10\delta_{2}}{\delta_{0}}}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H_{(\mu^{-1})}^{N_{0}}}.

We need to estimate the integral of the preceding expression on
t\in[0,\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})]
. By (
4.67
) from the proof of Proposition
4.10
, it follows that
\mu^{-1}\lesssim X(t;x_{0})
; hence it suffices to estimate the contribution of
X(t;x_{0})
. Using (
4.3
), (
4.20
) or (
4.31
) and (
6.13
), we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})}X(t;x_{0})\lambda_{0}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda)\left(\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\right)^{2}\lambda_{0}^{\frac{10\delta_{2}}{\delta_{0}}}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{N_{0}}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{M}\lambda_{0}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda_{0})\lambda_{0}^{-2+4\delta_{3}N_{0}+\frac{10\delta_{2}}{\delta_{0}}}(\log\lambda_{0})^{-4}\epsilon(\lambda_{0})^{-3}c_{x_{0}}^{-1}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{N_{0}}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\tau_{M}\frac{\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda_{0})}{\lambda_{0}^{1-\delta_{0}-3\sigma_{0}}}\lambda_{0}^{-\delta_{0}+4\delta_{3}N_{0}+\frac{10\delta_{2}}{\delta_{0}}+\frac{1}{2}\delta_{0}}(\log\lambda_{0})^{-4}c_{x_{0}}^{-1}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H^{N_{0}}_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}}

which is tightly acceptable thanks to (
1.19
).

Step 4: Contribution of
ip_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}
, remainder.

Now, we estimate the contribution of
ip_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{rem}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}
. Each term in (
3.8
) for
p=p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{rem}(x_{2},\xi_{2})P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}(\xi_{2})
is of the form
\int_{0}^{\sigma}{}^{(\Phi)}q(x_{2},\xi_{2};\sigma)\,\mathrm{d}\sigma
, where
q
obeys the hypothesis of Proposition
5.1
with

\lambda_{q}=\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}},\quad g(x_{2})=\|{\Gamma\partial_{x_{2}}f}\|_{L^{\infty}},\quad M(\xi_{2})=\lambda_{0}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{{\lambda^{2(1-\delta_{2})}}}+\frac{\mu^{2}\lambda}{{\lambda^{3(1-\delta_{2})}}}+\frac{\mu^{2}\lambda^{2}}{{\lambda^{4(1-\delta_{2})}}}\right).

Since
g(x_{2})
and
M(\xi_{2})
are constant, Proposition
5.1
(with
s=0
) immediately implies

\displaystyle\|{{}^{(\Phi)}r_{p_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{rem}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}},-2}\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\Gamma\partial_{x_{2}}f}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lambda_{0}\left(\frac{\mu^{2}}{{\lambda^{2(1-\delta_{2})}}}+\frac{\mu^{2}\lambda}{{\lambda^{3(1-\delta_{2})}}}+\frac{\mu^{2}\lambda^{2}}{{\lambda^{4(1-\delta_{2})}}}\right)\|{a}\|_{H_{\mu^{-1}}^{10000}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{\Gamma\partial_{x_{2}}f}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lambda_{0}\frac{\lambda_{0}^{10\delta_{2}+N_{0}\delta_{3}}}{\lambda_{0}^{2}}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H_{\mu^{-1}}^{N_{0}}},

which is acceptable.

Step 5: Contribution of
s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}
.

In this step, we handle the contribution of
s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}
. Each term in (
3.7
) for
p=s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}^{rem}(x_{2},\xi_{2})P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}(\xi_{2})
is of the form
\int_{0}^{\sigma}{}^{(\Phi)}q(x_{2},\xi_{2};\sigma)\,\mathrm{d}\sigma
, where
q
obeys the hypothesis of Proposition
5.1
with

\lambda_{q}=\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}},\quad g(x_{2})=g:=\|{\partial_{x_{2}}^{2}f}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|{\Gamma\partial_{x_{2}}^{2}f}\|_{L^{\infty}},\quad M(\xi_{2})=\lambda_{0}\frac{\xi_{2}}{|{\xi_{2}}|}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}^{\prime}(\xi_{2})\left(\frac{\mu}{{\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}}+\frac{\mu\lambda}{{\lambda^{2(1-\delta_{2})}}}\right).

In order to apply Proposition
5.1
, note that, by (
1.11
) and the almost comparability of
\gamma
and
\xi_{2}\partial_{\xi_{2}}\gamma
, we have
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{<\mu^{-1}}\lesssim M(\lambda)
if we choose
N=1000(1+\beta_{0})
. On the other hand,
g
is constant, so
\overline{g}_{<\mu^{-1}}=g
. Putting together the above observations with Propositions
3.3
and
5.1
(with
s=0
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\|{{}^{(\Phi)}r_{s_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}P_{>\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}},-1}\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim g\lambda_{0}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}^{\prime}(\lambda)\left(\frac{\mu}{{\lambda^{1-\delta_{2}}}}+\frac{\mu\lambda}{{\lambda^{2(1-\delta_{2})}}}\right)\|{a}\|_{H_{\mu^{-1}}^{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim g\lambda_{0}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}^{\prime}(\lambda)\left(\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\right)\lambda_{0}^{\frac{10\delta_{2}}{\delta_{0}}}\|{a_{0}}\|_{H_{\mu^{-1}}^{N_{0}}}.

To estimate the integral of the preceding expression on
t\in[0,\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})]
, we use (
4.3
) and (
6.14
) to estimate

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})}\lambda_{0}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}^{\prime}(\lambda)\left(\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\right)\lambda_{0}^{\frac{10\delta_{2}}{\delta_{0}}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\left(\frac{\mu}{\lambda}\right)\lambda_{0}^{\frac{10\delta_{2}}{\delta_{0}}}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{M\lambda_{0}}\frac{\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}^{\prime}(\lambda)}{\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda)}\,\mathrm{d}\lambda

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{1}{10}\delta_{0}+\frac{10\delta_{2}}{\delta_{0}}}\beta_{0}\log M\lesssim\delta_{0}^{-1}\beta_{0}\lambda_{0}^{-\frac{1}{3}-\frac{1}{10}\delta_{0}+\frac{10\delta_{2}}{\delta_{0}}}\log\lambda_{0},

which is acceptable.

Step 6: Contribution of
r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}
.

We begin by splitting

\displaystyle\|{r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\|{r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})P_{<\max\{\mu,\lambda_{0}\}}(D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\mathrel{\phantom{=}}+\sum_{\nu>\max\{\mu,\lambda_{0}\}}\|{r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})P_{\nu}(D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}.

For the first term, note that
\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\gamma(\lambda_{0},\max\{\mu,\lambda_{0}\})}r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},\xi_{2})P_{<\max\{\mu,\lambda_{0}\}}(\xi_{2})
is a classical symbol of order
0
. By the
L^{2}
-boundedness of its quantization, as well as Lemma
6.2
, we obtain the bound

\displaystyle\|{r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})P_{<\max\{\mu,\lambda_{0}\}}(D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{1}{\lambda_{0}}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\max\{\mu,\lambda_{0}\})\left(\frac{\max\{\mu,\lambda_{0}\}}{\lambda}\right)^{N}\|{a}\|_{H_{\mu^{-1}}^{N}},

which is acceptable if we take
N=\delta_{2}^{-1}(\beta_{0}+2)
, which is bounded by
N_{0}
.

For each summand in the second term, note that
\frac{\nu^{2}}{\lambda_{0}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\nu)}r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},\xi_{2})P_{\nu}(\xi_{2})
is a classical symbol of order
0
. As before, we therefore have

\displaystyle\|{r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})P_{\nu}(D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\nu^{2}}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\nu)\min\left\{\left(\frac{\nu}{\lambda}\right)^{N},\left(\frac{\lambda}{\nu}\right)^{N}\right\}\|{a}\|_{H_{\mu^{-1}}^{N}}.

By the slow variance assumption on
\gamma
, we have
\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\nu)\lesssim\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda)\max\{\left(\frac{\nu}{\lambda}\right)^{\beta_{0}},\left(\frac{\lambda}{\nu}\right)^{\beta_{0}}\}
.
Hence, applying the preceding bound with
N=1000(1+\beta_{0})
, we obtain the summed bound

\displaystyle\sum_{\nu>\mu}\|{r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})P_{\nu}(D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\lambda_{0}^{\delta_{2}}\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\lambda^{2}}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda)\|{a_{0}}\|_{H_{\mu^{-1}}^{N}}.

Then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})}\sum_{\nu>\mu}\|{r_{\mathring{\theta},\lambda_{0}}(x_{2},D_{2})P_{\nu}(D_{2})\widetilde{\psi}}\|_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{0}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})}\frac{\lambda_{0}}{\lambda^{2}}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda)\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\lesssim\lambda_{0}^{\delta_{2}}\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{M\lambda_{0}}\frac{1}{\lambda^{2}}\mathrm{d}\lambda\lesssim\lambda_{0}^{-1+\delta_{2}},

which is acceptable.

Finally, we consider the case
\Omega=\mathbb{T}^{2}
, in which case, by the
x_{2}
-periodicity of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}
,

\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}(x_{1},x_{2})=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}(\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}({}^{(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R})}\widetilde{\varphi}))(x_{1},x_{2}-k).

We repeat the above procedure, but now use the boundedness on
L^{2}(\langle{x_{2}}\rangle^{4}\mathrm{d}x_{2})
for classical pseudo-differential operators (for Step 1), and Proposition
5.1
with
X_{0}=X(t;x_{0})
and
s=2
for
\Phi
-conjugated remainders. Then we obtain the unit-scale-localized bounds

\int_{0}^{\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})}\|{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}({}^{(\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R})}\widetilde{\varphi})}\|_{L^{2}(k,k+1)}\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\min\left\{1,\frac{1}{k^{2}}\right\}\lambda_{0}^{-\delta_{6}}.

(6.18)

Summing up in
k
, the desired conclusion in the periodic case follows. ∎

7
Proof of illposedness

We now establish the illposedness theorems stated in Section
1.5
.

7.1
Linear illposedness

In this section, we prove Theorem
A
. As it is assumed in the statement of Theorem
A
, we are given a quadratically degenerate function
f
together with parameters
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
,
M>1
,
0<\delta_{0}<\frac{1}{100}
,
0\leq\sigma_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3}(1-2\delta_{0})
, so that the conditions (
1.18
)–(
1.20
) hold. We also set
c_{1}=\frac{1}{3}+\frac{1}{10}\delta_{0}
(cf. (
6.14
)). Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\mathring{x}_{2}=0
and
f^{\prime\prime}(0)<0
. We introduce parameters
\Lambda_{0}\geq 1
and
0<T_{0}\leq 1
, to be determined in the course of the proof, and require
\lambda_{0}\geq\Lambda_{0}
and
\tau_{M}\leq T_{0}
. We also fix the regularity exponents
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
.

We apply the construction of a degenerating wave packet in Sections
3
–
6
with
N_{0}=10000\max\{1+\beta_{0},1+|{s}|,1+|{s^{\prime}}|\}
and the above parameters; we take
\Lambda_{0}\geq\Lambda
and
T_{0}\leq T
. As a result, we obtain
7
7

7

Observe from Section
6.1
that while the constant
\mu
and bounds depend on the choice of
N_{0}
, the wave packet
\varphi
itself is independent of
N_{0}
.
a degenerating wave packet
\widetilde{\varphi}=\Re\left(ae^{i\mathbf{\Phi}}\right)
satisfying
\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}(\Omega)}=1
, where
\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}=\left.\widetilde{\varphi}\right|_{t=0}
. Introducing the shorthand

{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}:=\|{a_{0}}\|_{H_{(\mu_{0}^{-1})}^{N_{0}}},

note that
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\simeq 1
. Moreover, in view of Proposition
6.1
.(2) and the choice of
m_{0}
in Section
6.1
, we have, for any
s^{\prime\prime},\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
,

\lambda_{0}^{-s^{\prime\prime}}\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda_{0})^{-\sigma}\|{\Gamma^{\sigma}\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime\prime}}}\simeq_{s^{\prime\prime},\sigma}1.

(7.1)

We introduce
\phi_{0}
and
\varphi_{0}
defined by the relations

\phi_{0}(x)=\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{0}(x),\quad\varphi_{0}(x)=\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}(x).

(7.2)

We now proceed to prove (
1.22
).
Let
\phi
be an
\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{2}
-solution to
L_{\mathring{\theta}}\phi=0
on
\left[0,\frac{100}{99}t_{f}(\tau_{M})\right]
with
\left.\phi\right|_{t=0}=\phi_{0}
. Then
\varphi=\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi
is a
L^{2}
-solution to
\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\varphi=0
on the same interval with
\left.\varphi\right|_{t=0}=\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi_{0}=\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}(x)
. With the simplified notation
t^{\ast}=\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})
, note that
t^{\ast}\leq\frac{100}{99}t_{f}(\tau_{M})
by (
4.3
). In view of (
7.1
) and (
7.2
), it suffices to establish

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[0,t^{\ast}]}\|{\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi(t,\cdot)}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}\geq C_{s^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(M\lambda_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}M^{s^{\prime}}\lambda_{0}^{s^{\prime}}\end{split}

for any
s^{\prime}>0
.

Recall that
\widetilde{\varphi}=\Re(e^{i\mathbf{\Phi}}a)
is well-defined on
\left[0,t^{\ast}\right]
and belongs to
C^{\infty}_{c}(\Omega)
. Therefore, the generalized energy inequality

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle-\langle{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle-\langle{\varphi,\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle\right|\lesssim\|{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}}\end{split}

can be justified on the time interval
\left[0,t^{\ast}\right]
. Therefore, we immediately obtain the inequality

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle\right|\lesssim(\|{\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}})\|{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Integrating in time and applying the error estimate (
6.17
) gives

\begin{split}\left|\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(t)-\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(0)\right|&\lesssim\int_{0}^{t}(\|{\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}})\|{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\\
&\lesssim(t\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}+\lambda_{0}^{-\delta_{6}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|})\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\end{split}

for any
0<t\leq t^{\ast}
. In the last inequality we have used
\|{\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}
and
\|{\varphi}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,t^{\ast}];L^{2})}\lesssim\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}
. Note that in the above inequalities, the implicit constants depend only on
f,\gamma
. Therefore, taking
\Lambda_{0}
larger and the constant
T_{0}
smaller if necessary (the latter in a way depending only on
f,\gamma
), we may guarantee that

\begin{split}\left|\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(t^{\ast})-\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(0)\right|\leq\frac{1}{4}\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

which gives, recalling
\varphi|_{t=0}=\widetilde{\varphi}|_{t=0}=\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}
,

\begin{split}\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(t^{\ast})>\frac{1}{4}\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Furthermore, from
\widetilde{\varphi}=\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}+\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}
and the estimate for
\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}
in (
6.5
) and (
6.14
),

\begin{split}\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}}\rangle(t^{\ast})&\geq\frac{1}{4}\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}-\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}(t^{\ast})}\|_{L^{2}}\\
&\geq\frac{1}{4}\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}-C\lambda_{0}^{-c_{1}}\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\geq\frac{1}{8}\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

by again taking
\Lambda_{0}
larger if necessary. Combining this inequality with
\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}=1
,

\begin{split}\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}}\rangle(t^{\ast})\leq\|{\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi(t^{\ast})}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}\|{\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}(t^{\ast})}\|_{H^{-s^{\prime}}},\end{split}

and (
6.4
), we conclude that

\begin{split}\|{\phi(t^{\ast})}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}=\|{\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi(t^{\ast})}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}\geq C_{s^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(\lambda(t^{\ast}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}\lambda(t^{\ast})^{s^{\prime}}\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\geq C_{s^{\prime}}\frac{1}{\gamma_{\lambda_{0}}(M\lambda_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}}M^{s^{\prime}}\lambda_{0}^{s^{\prime}}.\end{split}

In the last inequality, we have used (
1.21
) and that
\lambda(t^{\ast})=M\lambda_{0}
(see Lemma
4.3
). This completes the proof of Theorem
A
. ∎

7.2
Linear illposedness, dissipative case

In this section, we proceed to prove Theorem
B
. Let
f_{0}
be a smooth function satisfying the assumptions in the statement of Theorem
B
. Without loss of generality, we further assume that
f^{\prime\prime}_{0}(0)<0
. Recall that
f(t,x_{2})
for
t\geq 0
was defined to be the solution of
\partial_{t}f+\kappa\Upsilon f=0
. From the assumption that the symbol of
\Upsilon
is even, we have that
f(t,\cdot)
is even as well. In particular,
f^{\prime}(t,0)=0
for all
t\geq 0
. We are also given parameters
\lambda_{0}\in\mathbb{N}
,
M>1
,
0<\delta_{1}\leq\delta_{0}<\frac{1}{100}
and
0\leq\sigma_{0}\leq\frac{1}{3}(1-2\delta_{0})
, so that the conditions (
1.18
)–(
1.20
) as well as (
1.24
)–(
1.25
) hold. We also set
c_{1}=\frac{1}{3}+\frac{1}{10}\delta_{0}
(cf. (
6.14
)). Let
\Lambda_{1}\geq 1
and
0<T_{1}\leq 1
be parameters to be determined below, and we also require that
\lambda_{0}\geq\Lambda_{1}
and
\tau_{M}\leq T_{1}
. Fix also the regularity exponents
s,s^{\prime}\in\mathbb{R}
.

We apply the construction in Sections
3
–
6
with
N_{0}=10^{4}\max\{1+\beta_{0},1+\alpha_{0},1+|{s}|,1+|{s^{\prime}}|\}
and the above parameters; we take
\Lambda_{1}\leq\Lambda
and
T_{1}\leq T
. As in the proof of Theorem
A
, we obtain a
L^{2}
-normalized degenerating wave packet
\widetilde{\varphi}=\Re(ae^{i\mathbf{\Phi}})
; we set
\phi_{0}=\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}
, where
\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}=\left.\widetilde{\varphi}\right|_{t=0}
.

Let
\phi
be a
\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}L^{2}
-solution to
L_{\mathring{\theta}}^{(\kappa)}\phi=0
on
\left[0,\frac{100}{99}t_{f}(\tau_{M})\right]
with
\phi|_{t=0}=\phi_{0}
, so that
\varphi:=\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\phi
is a
L^{2}
-solution to
\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}^{(\kappa)}\varphi=0
with
\left.\varphi\right|_{t=0}=\varphi_{0}
. We also introduce
t^{\ast}=\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M})
, which obeys
t^{\ast}\leq\frac{100}{99}t_{f}(\tau_{M})
. As before, we apply the generalized energy inequality to
\varphi
and
\widetilde{\varphi}
and obtain

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle-\langle{\mathcal{L}^{(\kappa)}_{\mathring{\theta}}\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle-\langle{\varphi,\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}^{(\kappa)}\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle\right|\lesssim\|{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}},\end{split}

which is valid on the time interval
\left[0,t^{\ast}\right]
. This time, we obtain the inequality

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle\right|\lesssim(\|{\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}}+\kappa\|{\Upsilon\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}})\|{\varphi}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(7.3)

Since
N_{0}\geq 10^{4}(1+\alpha_{0})
, arguing as in the proof of Proposition
6.1
, we have

\kappa\|{\Upsilon\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\kappa\upsilon(\lambda_{0},\lambda){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|},

where
{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}
is defined as in the proof of Theorem
A
.
Applying the previous bound to (
7.3
) and proceeding as in the inviscid case gives

\begin{split}\left|\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(t)-\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(0)\right|&\lesssim\left(t\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}+\left(\lambda_{0}^{-\delta_{6}}+\int_{0}^{t}|\upsilon(\lambda_{0},\lambda(t^{\prime}))|\,\mathrm{d}t^{\prime}\right){|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\right)\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\end{split}

for any
0<t\leq t^{\ast}
. Taking
t=t^{\ast}
and using
\dot{\lambda}=(1-\epsilon)|{f^{\prime\prime}(0,0)}|\lambda_{0}\gamma(\lambda_{0},\lambda)
with a change of variables, we have that

\begin{split}\int_{0}^{t^{\ast}}|\upsilon(\lambda_{0},\lambda(t))|\,\mathrm{d}t\lesssim\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{M\lambda_{0}}\frac{\upsilon(\lambda_{0},\lambda)}{\gamma(\lambda_{0},\lambda)}\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{\lambda_{0}}.\end{split}

Under the assumption (
1.24
), taking
\Lambda_{1}
larger if necessary, we can conclude that

\begin{split}\langle{\varphi,\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(t^{\ast})\geq\frac{1}{16}\|{\varphi_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

The rest of the proof is identical to the inviscid case. ∎

7.3
Nonlinear illposedness

In this section, we will establish Theorem
C
. We only consider the inviscid case, as the dissipative case can be treated similarly.

We assume the same hypothesis and conventions as in the proof of Theorem
A
in the beginning of Section
7.1
. Given
\epsilon>0
and
\mathring{\theta}=f(x_{2})
in the statement of the Theorem
C
, we consider the sequence

\begin{split}{\vartheta_{0}^{(\lambda_{0})}}:=c\epsilon{\gamma(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{0})^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{0}^{-s}\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\in C^{\infty}_{c}(\Omega),\end{split}

where
\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}=\Re(e^{i\mathbf{\Phi}}a)|_{t=0}
is the
L^{2}
-normalized degenerating wave packet at the initial time with frequency
\lambda_{0}
from the proof of Theorem
A
. Here,
c>0
is an absolute constant inserted to guarantee that

\begin{split}\|{{\vartheta_{0}^{(\lambda_{0})}}}\|_{H^{s}}\leq\epsilon\end{split}

uniformly for
\lambda_{0}\geq\Lambda_{0}
(the same
\Lambda_{0}
from Theorem
A
), as it is required in the statement of the Theorem
C
. We then consider the sequence initial data

\begin{split}\theta^{(\lambda_{0})}|_{t=0}=\mathring{\theta}+{\vartheta_{0}^{(\lambda_{0})}}\end{split}

with
\lambda_{0}\geq\Lambda_{0}
for (
1.1
). Recalling the parameters
\delta,A>0
given in the statement, we may assume towards a contradiction that for any sufficiently large
\lambda_{0}
, there exists a solution
\theta^{(\lambda_{0})}
to (
1.1
) with initial data
\theta^{(\lambda_{0})}|_{t=0}
satisfying
\theta^{(\lambda_{0})}-\mathring{\theta}\in L^{\infty}([0,\delta];H^{s^{\prime}})
and

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\|{(\theta^{(\lambda_{0})}-\mathring{\theta})(t,\cdot)}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}\leq A.\end{split}

From now on, we shall fix some large
\lambda_{0}\geq\Lambda_{0}
and omit writing out the dependence of
\lambda_{0}
on the solution. On the time interval
[0,\delta]
, we simply define

\begin{split}\varphi^{\star}(t,\cdot)=\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}(\theta^{(\lambda_{0})}-\mathring{\theta})(t,\cdot).\end{split}

Since
\theta^{(\lambda_{0})}
and
\mathring{\theta}
are solutions to (
1.1
), we see that the equation for
\varphi^{\star}
is given by

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\varphi^{\star}=-\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\left(\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}\right)=:\mathcal{N}[\varphi^{\star}].\end{split}

We claim that

\displaystyle\langle{\varphi^{\star}_{0},\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\rangle

\displaystyle\simeq\|{\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}},

(7.4)

\displaystyle\|{\mathcal{N}[\varphi^{\star}]}\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+A)\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}.

(7.5)

Bound (
7.4
) follows from Proposition
6.1
.(2).
To prove (
7.5
), we use
s^{\prime}>\frac{3}{2}\beta_{0}+3
. We take some
0<\varepsilon<s^{\prime}-(3+\beta_{0})
and estimate

\begin{split}\|{\mathcal{N}[\varphi^{\star}]}\|_{L^{2}}&\leq C\|{\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}}\|_{H^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}}\\
&\leq C(\|{\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}}\|_{H^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}}\|{\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{\infty}}+\|{\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}}\|_{H^{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}})\\
&\leq C_{\varepsilon}\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\frac{\beta_{0}+1}{s^{\prime}}}\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}^{\frac{\beta_{0}+1}{s^{\prime}}}\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\frac{2+\varepsilon}{s^{\prime}}}\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}^{\frac{2+\varepsilon}{s^{\prime}}}\\
&\qquad+C_{\varepsilon}\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\frac{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}+1}{s^{\prime}}}\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}^{\frac{\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}+1}{s^{\prime}}}\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}^{1-\frac{2+\varepsilon+\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}{s^{\prime}}}\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}^{\frac{2+\varepsilon+\frac{\beta_{0}}{2}}{s^{\prime}}}\\
&\leq C_{\varepsilon}(1+A)\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

In this chain of estimates, we used
\|{\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}h}\|_{H^{s^{\prime\prime}}}\lesssim_{s^{\prime\prime}}\|{h}\|_{H^{s^{\prime\prime}+\frac{\beta}{2}}}
and
\|{\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}h}\|_{H^{s^{\prime\prime}}}\lesssim_{s^{\prime\prime}}\|{h}\|_{H^{s^{\prime\prime}}}
, which follow the assumptions on
\Gamma
and Littlewood–Paley decomposition, as well as the Sobolev product estimate and interpolation estimate. This verifies the claim.

Using the energy structure of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}
, as well as the cancellation
\langle{\mathcal{N}[\varphi^{\star}],\varphi^{\star}}\rangle=0
, we obtain

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\langle{\varphi^{\star},\varphi^{\star}}\rangle\right|\lesssim{\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}.\end{split}

Hence, by Gronwall’s inquality,

\begin{split}\|{\varphi^{\star}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}\leq\|{\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\exp({Ct})\end{split}

(7.6)

for
0\leq t\leq\delta
with
C>0
depending only on
f
and
\Gamma
. Using the generalized energy inequality together with (
7.5
) and (
7.6
),

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\langle{\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle\right|&\lesssim(\|{\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}})(\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathcal{N}[\varphi^{\star}]}\|_{L^{2}})\\
&\lesssim(1+A)\exp({Ct})(\|{\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}}\|_{L^{2}})\|{\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(7.7)

Taking
t\leq{\delta}
and integrating in time, we obtain similarly as in the proof of linear illposedness that

\begin{split}\langle{\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(t)\geq\langle{\varphi^{\star}_{0},\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\rangle-C(1+A)\left(t\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}+\lambda_{0}^{-\delta_{6}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}\right)\|{\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

(7.8)

At this point, from the definition of
H^{s}
-
H^{s^{\prime}}
instability (Definition
1.12
), we have a sequence
(M_{(n)},\lambda_{0(n)})
satisfying the growth conditions (
1.18
)–(
1.20
) (for some
\sigma_{0(n)}
),
\tau_{M_{(n)}}\to 0
, and
\frac{\gamma(\lambda_{0(n)},\lambda_{0(n)})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\gamma(\lambda_{0(n)},M_{(n)}\lambda_{0(n)})^{\frac{1}{2}}}M_{(n)}^{s^{\prime}}\lambda_{0(n)}^{s^{\prime}-s}\to\infty
. Therefore, by taking
n
to be sufficiently large, we have

\begin{split}t^{\ast}_{(n)}:=\frac{1}{1-\epsilon}t_{f}(\tau_{M_{(n)}})<\min\{\delta,\tfrac{1}{10C(1+A)}\},\quad C(1+A)\lambda_{0(n)}^{-\delta_{6}}{|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|\widetilde{\varphi}_{0}|\kern-1.07639pt|\kern-1.07639pt|}<\frac{1}{10}.\end{split}

Here,
C
is the constant from (
7.8
) which is independent of
\lambda_{0}
. From (
7.8
), we now deduce that

\begin{split}\langle{\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(t^{\ast}_{(n)})\geq\frac{1}{2}\|{\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

By Proposition
6.1
, Cauchy–Schwartz and the
H^{s^{\prime}}
-
H^{-s^{\prime}}
duality, we may decompose

\begin{split}\langle{\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}}\rangle(t^{\ast}_{(n)})\leq\|{\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}(t^{\ast}_{(n)})}\|_{H^{s}}\|{\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}(t^{\ast}_{(n)})}\|_{H^{-s}}+\|{\varphi^{\star}(t^{\ast}_{(n)})}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}(t^{\ast}_{(n)})}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

where

\begin{split}\|{\varphi^{\star}(t^{\ast}_{(n)})}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}(t^{\ast}_{(n)})}\|_{L^{2}}<\frac{1}{10}\|{\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

By (
6.4
) in Proposition
6.1
, we obtain

\begin{split}\|{\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}(t^{\ast}_{(n)})}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}&\gtrsim\frac{\|{\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}}{\|{\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}(t^{\ast}_{(n)})}\|_{H^{-{s^{\prime}}}}}\gtrsim\epsilon\frac{\gamma(\lambda_{0(n)},\lambda_{0(n)})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\gamma(\lambda_{0(n)},M_{(n)}\lambda_{0(n)})^{\frac{1}{2}}}M_{(n)}^{s^{\prime}}\lambda_{0(n)}^{s^{\prime}-s}.\end{split}

(7.9)

By
H^{s}
-
H^{s^{\prime}}
instability (see Definition
1.12
), the RHS becomes arbitrary large as
n\to\infty
. On the other hand, using the definition of
\varphi^{\star}
, we obtain

\begin{split}A\geq\|{\theta(t^{\ast}_{(n)})}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}=\|{\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}(t^{\ast}_{(n)})+\mathring{\theta}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}\geq\|{\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}(t^{\ast}_{(n)})}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}-\|{\mathring{\theta}}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}\end{split}

which is a contradiction with (
7.9
) as we take
n\to\infty
. The proof is now complete.
∎

7.4
Nonexistence

The goal of this section is to prove Theorem
D
. We proceed in several steps.

1. Choice of the initial data
. To begin with, we fix some
f_{0}(x_{2})
satisfying the following properties:

•

f_{0}
is
C^{\infty}
smooth and supported in
[-2,2]
;

•

f_{0}(x_{2})=-\frac{1}{2}x_{2}^{2}
for
|x_{2}|\leq 1
.

For
\lambda_{0}\geq\Lambda_{0}
, let us denote by
\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0})}(t)
the degenerate wave packet solution adapted to
f_{0}
with frequency
\lambda_{0}
, normalized in
L^{2}
. Furthermore, we assume that the wave packet at the initial time is supported in
(\frac{1}{2},1]
. For convenience, we recall a few essential properties of
\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0})}(t)
. Define
M(\lambda_{0}):=\min\left\{\gamma(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{0})^{1-\delta_{0}},\lambda_{0}^{\delta_{0}},\,\gamma(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{0})^{\frac{1-2\delta_{0}}{\beta_{0}}},\lambda_{0}^{\frac{1-5\delta_{0}}{3\beta_{0}}}\right\}
and
\tau(\lambda_{0}):=\int_{\lambda_{0}}^{M(\lambda_{0})\lambda_{0}}\frac{1}{\gamma(\lambda_{0},\lambda)}\frac{\mathrm{d}\lambda}{\lambda_{0}}
.

•

Degeneration estimate: there is a decomposition
\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0})}=\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0}),main}+\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0}),small}
such that for
t\in[0,\tau(\lambda_{0})]
,

\begin{split}\|{\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0}),main}(t)}\|_{H^{-s}}\leq C_{-s}\gamma(\lambda_{0},\lambda(t))^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda(t)^{-s},\qquad\|{\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0}),small}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\lambda(t)^{-c_{1}}.\end{split}

•

Error estimate:
\|{\mathcal{L}_{f_{0}}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0})}}\|_{L^{1}([0,\frac{100}{99}t_{f_{0}}(\tau_{M})];L^{2})}\leq C\lambda_{0}^{-\delta_{6}}
and
\|{\mathcal{L}_{f_{0}}\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0})}}\|_{L^{1}([0,\frac{100}{99}t_{f_{0}}(\tau_{M})];L^{2}[k,k+1])}\leq C\min\{1,k^{-2}\}\lambda_{0}^{-\delta_{6}}
(see (
6.17
) and (
6.18
)).

By
\lambda(t)
, we mean the solution of (
4.16
) with
f=f_{0}
, which verifies
\lambda(\tau(\lambda_{0}))\geq M(\lambda_{0})\lambda_{0}
. In the above, it is important that the positive constants
C
,
c_{1}
and
\delta_{6}
do not depend on
\lambda_{0}
.

We now take the following shear steady state:

\begin{split}\mathring{\theta}(x_{2})=\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}f_{k}:=\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}a_{k}f_{0}(x_{2}-y_{k})\end{split}

where
\{a_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
can be any square summable sequence;
we fix it to be
a_{k}=k^{-2}
for simplicity. On the other hand,
\{y_{k}\}_{k\geq 1}
is a strictly increasing sequence to be determined;
y_{1}=1
and we shall take
y_{k}
sufficiently large with respect to
y_{k-1}
for
k\geq 2
. By taking
k_{0}\geq 1
large, it is guaranteed that
\|{\mathring{\theta}}\|_{H^{s}}<\frac{\epsilon}{2}
for any given
\epsilon>0
and
s
.

Next, we consider the perturbation

\begin{split}{\vartheta_{0}}={\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}_{0}}:=\sum_{k=k_{0}}^{\infty}a_{k}\gamma(\lambda_{0,k},\lambda_{0,k})^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{0,k}^{-s}\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0,k}},\end{split}

where

{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0,k}:=\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0,k})}(t=0,x_{2}-y_{k}),}

and
\lambda_{0,k}\geq\Lambda_{0}
is a strictly increasing sequence to be determined below. Observe that (using a simple rescaling in time)
\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0,k})}(a_{k}t,x_{2}-y_{k})
is simply the wave packet adapted to the rescaled and translated profile
f_{k}
with frequency
\lambda_{0,k}
. By taking larger
k_{0}
if necessary, we can guarantee that
\|{\vartheta_{0}}\|_{H^{s}}<\frac{\epsilon}{2}
.

We then take the initial data

\begin{split}\theta_{0}=\mathring{\theta}_{0}+\vartheta_{0}\end{split}

for (
1.1
), which satisfies
\|{\theta_{0}}\|_{H^{s}}<\epsilon
. Towards a contradiction, we assume that there exist
\delta>0
and a solution
\theta\in L^{\infty}([0,\delta];H^{s})
to (
1.1
) with
\theta(t=0)=\theta_{0}
. We denote

\begin{split}A=\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\|{\theta(t)}\|_{H^{s}}\end{split}

and define on
t\in[0,\delta]

\begin{split}\vartheta(t):=\theta(t)-\mathring{\theta},\qquad\varphi^{\star}(t):=\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\vartheta(t).\end{split}

In what follows, we shall often suppress the dependence of implicit constants on
\Gamma
,
f_{0}
and
s
. We remark that, logically, the sequence
\{\lambda_{0,k}\}
shall be fixed first, and then shall
\{y_{0,k}\}
be fixed – the last part crucially uses the unboundedness of
\Omega=\mathbb{T}\times\mathbb{R}
.

2. Localization of the energy identity
. We first introduce some cutoff functions. Let
\chi\geq 0
be a smooth function supported on
[-1,1]
and satisfies
\chi=1
on
[-\frac{1}{2},\frac{1}{2}]
. Assuming that
y_{k-1}
is given for some
k\geq 2
, we take
y_{k}\geq 8y_{k-1}
and
\chi_{k}(x_{2}):=\chi(2y_{k}^{-1}(x_{2}-y_{k}))
. It is then guaranteed that the support of
\chi_{k}
is disjoint from each other.

Recall that the equation for
\varphi^{\star}
is given by

\begin{split}\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\varphi^{\star}+\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star})=0.\end{split}

As in Section
7.3
, testing the equation against
\varphi^{\star}
, we immediately obtain

\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}

which implies, by Gronwall’s inequality,

\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|{\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\exp(Ct).

(7.10)

Moreover, multiplying the equation by
\chi_{k}
and testing against
\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}
, we have from

\begin{split}&\langle{-\chi_{k}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{\perp}\mathring{\theta}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}+\chi_{k}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma\mathring{\theta}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star},\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\rangle\\
&\qquad=\langle{-\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{\perp}\mathring{\theta}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}(\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star})+\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma\mathring{\theta}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}),\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\rangle\\
&\qquad\quad+\langle{-\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{\perp}\mathring{\theta}\cdot[\chi_{k},\nabla\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}]\varphi^{\star}+\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma\mathring{\theta}\cdot[\chi_{k},\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}]\varphi^{\star},\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\rangle\\
&\qquad\quad+\langle{-[\chi_{k},\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}]\nabla^{\perp}\mathring{\theta}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}+[\chi_{k},\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}]\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma\mathring{\theta}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star},\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\rangle,\end{split}

and

\begin{split}\langle{\chi_{k}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}),\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\rangle&=\langle{[\chi_{k},\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}](\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}),\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\rangle\\
&\qquad+\langle{[\chi_{k},\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}]\varphi^{\star}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star},\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}(\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star})}\rangle\end{split}

that

\begin{split}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\lesssim_{\mathring{\theta},\Gamma}\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+{A}\|{\chi_{k}^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Here, it is important that the implicit constant depends only on
\mathring{\theta}
and
\Gamma
and is independent of
k
. We remark that we have used the commutator bounds
\|{[g,\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}]h}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}\lesssim_{s^{\prime},\Gamma}\|{g^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{h}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}+\beta_{0}-1}}
and
\|{[g,\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}]h}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}\lesssim_{s^{\prime},\Gamma}\|{g^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{h}\|_{H^{s^{\prime}}}
, which may be established using the assumptions on
\Gamma
, Littlewood–Paley decomposition and writing out the commutator using the integral kernel of
\Gamma^{\pm\frac{1}{2}}P_{\nu}
; we omit the standard details. Regarding
\mathring{\theta}
, this estimate requires
\|{\Gamma\nabla\mathring{\theta}}\|_{L^{\infty}}<\infty
, and here it suffices to have that
s>\beta_{0}+2
.

Requiring that
y_{k}
satisfies

y_{k}^{-1}\lesssim a_{k}\gamma(\lambda_{0,k},\lambda_{0,k})^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{0,k}^{-s}

for suitably chosen implicit constant independent of
k
, we may ensure that

\|{\chi_{k}^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\leq\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}.

By Gronwall’s inequality, we conclude the following
localized energy estimate
:

\begin{split}\sup_{t\in[0,\delta]}\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}{\exp(C(1+A)t)}.\end{split}

(7.11)

3. Localization of the generalized energy identity
. We denote

\begin{split}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}(t,x_{2}):=\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0,k})}(a_{k}t,x_{2}-y_{k}).\end{split}

From the properties of
\widetilde{\varphi}^{(\lambda_{0})}
summarized in the above, we have that with

\begin{split}M_{k}:=M(\lambda_{0,k}),\qquad\tau_{k}:=\tau(\lambda_{0,k})\end{split}

•

for
t\in[0,a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k}]
,
\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}(t)
is supported on
[y_{k},y_{k}+1]
and satisfies
\|{\widetilde{\varphi}(t)}\|_{L^{\infty}_{t}([0,a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k}];L^{2})}\leq C
;

•

there is a decomposition
\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}=\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}_{k}+\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}_{k}
such that for
t\in[0,a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k}]
,

\begin{split}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}_{k}(t)}\|_{H^{-s}}\leq C_{-s}\lambda_{k}^{-s}(a_{k}t),\qquad\|{\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}_{k}(t)}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C\lambda_{k}^{-c_{0}}(a_{k}t);\end{split}

•

we have
\|{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\|_{L^{1}([0,a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k}];L^{2})}\leq C\lambda_{0,k}^{-c_{0}}
and
\|{\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\|_{L^{1}([0,a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k}];L^{2}[\ell,\ell+1])}\leq C\min\{1,\ell^{-2}\}\lambda_{0,k}^{-c_{0}}
.

Here,
\lambda_{k}(t)
is the solution to (
4.16
) with
\lambda_{0}=\lambda_{0,k}
and
f=\mathring{\theta}
. The constants
C,C_{-s}
do not depend on
k
; in particular, the
L^{1}_{t}L^{2}
estimates follow directly from scaling property of
\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}
(
2.1
).
In particular, on
t\in[0,\min\{\delta,a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k}\}]
, we have
\chi_{k}^{2}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}(t)=\chi_{k}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}(t)=\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}(t)
. Before we proceed, note

\begin{split}\tau_{k}{\leq\frac{M_{k}}{\gamma(\lambda_{0,k},\lambda_{0,k})}\leq}\frac{1}{\gamma^{\delta_{0}}(\lambda_{0,k},\lambda_{0,k})}\end{split}

and therefore by taking
\lambda_{0,k}
sufficiently large, we may ensure that
a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k}\leq\delta
. Choosing
\lambda_{0,k}
larger if necessary, we may also ensure that
a_{k}M_{k}^{s-\beta_{0}}\to\infty
as
k\to\infty
.

Denoting
\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k}:=\mathcal{L}_{\mathring{\theta}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}
, we deduce from applying (
2.5
) with
\varphi=\varphi^{\star}
and
\psi=\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}
that

\begin{split}\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle&=\langle{\varphi^{\star},\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k}}\rangle+\langle{\varphi^{\star},\left([\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}},\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma\mathring{\theta}\cdot\nabla]\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}[\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}},\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma\mathring{\theta}\cdot\nabla]\right)(\chi_{k}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k})}\rangle\\
&\qquad-\langle{\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}),\chi_{k}^{2}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle.\end{split}

(7.12)

We write

\begin{split}\langle{\varphi^{\star},\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k}}\rangle=\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k}}\rangle+\langle{\varphi^{\star},(1-\chi_{k})\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k}}\rangle\end{split}

and apply Cauchy–Schwartz. Furthermore, modulo several commutators involving
\chi_{k}
, the other two terms on the right hand side of (
7.12
) can be written as

\begin{split}\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\left([\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}},\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma\mathring{\theta}\cdot\nabla]\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}+\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}[\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}},\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma\mathring{\theta}\cdot\nabla]\right)\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle-\langle{\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}(\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star})\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star})),\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle.\end{split}

Now, it is important that we have

\begin{split}\|{\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}(\nabla^{\perp}\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}(\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star})\cdot\nabla\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}(\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}))}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim(1+A)\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Observe that the terms involving commutators can be bounded in absolute value by

\begin{split}\lesssim(1+A^{2})\|{\chi_{k}^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim(1+A^{2})\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

Therefore, we have arrived at the following localized and generalized energy estimate:

\begin{split}\left|\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}t}\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle\right|&\lesssim(1+A^{2})(\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k}}\|_{L^{2}})(\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}+\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}){+\|{\varphi^{\star}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{(1-\chi_{k})\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k}}\|_{L^{2}}.}\end{split}

(7.13)

4. Conclusion
. Applying (
7.10
), (
7.12
) to (
7.13
) and integrating in time, we obtain that

\begin{split}\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle(t)&\geq\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle(0)\\
&\mathrel{\phantom{=}}-C(1+A^{2}){\exp(C(1+A)t)}\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}(t\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{0,k}}\|_{L^{2}}+\int_{0}^{t}\|{\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k}(\tau)}\|_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau)\\
&\mathrel{\phantom{=}}{-C\exp(Ct)\|{\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\|{(1-\chi_{k})\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k}(\tau)}\|_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau.}\end{split}

By Proposition
6.1
.(2) and our construction, observe that

\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle(0)\simeq\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\lesssim a_{k}\gamma(\lambda_{0,k},\lambda_{0,k})^{\frac{1}{2}}\lambda_{0,k}^{-s}.

We fix
t=a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k}
. Using the localized error bound, if we take
y_{k}
large enough depending on
\lambda_{0,k}
, we may ensure that

\|{\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\int_{0}^{t}\|{(1-\chi_{k})\boldsymbol{\epsilon}_{k}(\tau)}\|_{L^{2}}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\leq\lambda_{0,k}^{-c_{1}}\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle(0).

For
k
large enough so that
\tau_{k}\ll(1+A^{2})^{-1}
and
\lambda_{0,k}^{-c_{1}}\ll(1+A^{2})^{-1}
, we obtain

\begin{split}\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})\geq\frac{1}{2}\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle(0).\end{split}

On the other hand, we may bound

\begin{split}\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})\leq\|{\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})}\|_{H^{s}}\|{\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\varphi}^{main}_{k}(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})}\|_{H^{-{s}}}+\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}_{k}(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})}\|_{L^{2}}.\end{split}

By taking
k
larger if necessary, we may guarantee that

\begin{split}\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}^{small}_{k}(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})}\|_{L^{2}}<\frac{1}{4}\langle{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star},\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}}\rangle(0).\end{split}

This gives

\begin{split}\|{\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})}\|_{H^{s}}&\gtrsim\frac{\|{\chi_{k}\varphi^{\star}_{0}}\|_{L^{2}}\|{\widetilde{\varphi}_{k,0}}\|_{L^{2}}}{\|{\Gamma^{\frac{1}{2}}\widetilde{\varphi}_{k}^{main}(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})}\|_{H^{-{s}}}}\gtrsim{a_{k}}\frac{\gamma(\lambda_{0,k})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\lambda_{0,k}^{s}}\frac{\lambda^{s}_{k}(\tau_{k})}{\gamma(\lambda_{k}(\tau_{k}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}\\
&\gtrsim{a_{k}}\frac{\gamma(\lambda_{0,k})^{\frac{1}{2}}}{\gamma(\lambda_{k}(\tau_{k}))^{\frac{1}{2}}}M_{k}^{s}\gtrsim{a_{k}}M_{k}^{s-\beta_{0}}.\end{split}

For
k
large,
\|{[\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}},\chi_{k}]\varphi^{\star}}\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim(1+A)\|{\chi_{k}^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}}\lesssim 1
and therefore we obtain

\begin{split}\|{\chi_{k}(\theta(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})-\mathring{\theta})}\|_{H^{s}}=\|{\chi_{k}\Gamma^{-\frac{1}{2}}\varphi^{\star}(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})}\|_{H^{s}}\gtrsim{a_{k}}M_{k}^{s-\beta_{0}}.\end{split}

On the other hand, with a constant independent of
k
,

\begin{split}\|{\chi_{k}(\theta(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})-\mathring{\theta})}\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim\|{(\theta(a_{k}^{-1}\tau_{k})-\mathring{\theta})}\|_{H^{s}}\lesssim 1+A.\end{split}

We obtain a contradiction as
k\to\infty
since
{a_{k}M_{k}^{s-\beta_{0}}\to\infty}
. This finishes the proof. ∎
